Continuing work from thread T-019b9cbb-c371-7048-b075-b5b202595bfd. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@src/runtime/app.rs @src/runtime/mouse.rs @src/runtime/mod.rs @src/view/hit_test.rs @src/view/mod.rs @src/view/geometry.rs

I have completed the refactoring of the mouse event handling system.
I introduced a new `hit_test.rs` module in `src/view` with core types: `Point`, `MouseEvent`, `HitTarget` (an enum with 11 variants for different UI regions), and `EventResult`.
I then implemented pure hit-testing functions (`hit_test_modal`, `hit_test_sidebar`, etc.) and a main `hit_test_ui` dispatcher in `src/view/hit_test.rs`.
I created a new `mouse.rs` module in `src/runtime` to centralize mouse event dispatch. This module includes a `ClickTracker` for double/triple-click detection, `make_mouse_event` to construct events, and `handle_mouse_press` as the main entry point.
I implemented `handle_left_click` and `handle_middle_click` in `src/runtime/mouse.rs` to cover all `HitTarget` variants exhaustively, including specific logic for closing previews/tabs, starting rectangle selection, and handling sidebar/CSV interactions.
I updated `handle_mouse_press` to return a `MousePressResult` which includes a `Cmd` and a `start_drag_tracking` boolean, indicating if a text selection drag should be initiated.
I have now fully integrated this new system into `src/runtime/app.rs` by replacing the old `WindowEvent::MouseInput` handlers for left and middle clicks with calls to `handle_mouse_press`.
I also removed all the dead code, unused fields, and imports from `src/runtime/app.rs` and `src/runtime/mouse.rs` that were left behind by the refactoring.
The `ClickTracker` is now used in `app.rs` to manage click counts.
The `is_in_modal` function in `src/view/geometry.rs` and `tab_at_position` in `src/view/mod.rs` were marked with `#[allow(dead_code)]` as they are now used indirectly or might be used in the future.
All builds, lints, and tests are passing.

The `ensure_cursor_visible` logic is implemented across several layers, primarily in the text editor and CSV components. The core logic uses a `scroll_padding` value (default 1) and various `ScrollRevealMode` strategies to adjust the `Viewport`.

### Text Editor (Main)
The primary logic is in `src/model/editor.rs`, where `ensure_cursor_visible_with_mode` calculates vertical and horizontal safe zones based on `scroll_padding`.
- [src/model/editor.rs#L701-L784](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor.rs#L701-L784) - Implementation of `ensure_cursor_visible` and reveal modes (Minimal, TopAligned, BottomAligned, Centered).
- [src/model/editor.rs#L327](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor.rs#L327) - Definition of `scroll_padding`.
- [src/model/mod.rs#L616-L637](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/mod.rs#L616-L637) - High-level model wrappers that include `sync_preview_scroll`.
- [src/update/editor.rs#L53-L73](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/editor.rs#L53-L73) - Viewport adjustment triggered after cursor movement.

### CSV and Sidebar
- [src/csv/viewport.rs#L29-L50](file://~/code/token-editor/src/csv/viewport.rs#L29-L50) - CSV-specific `ensure_visible` implementation for grid navigation.
- [src/csv/navigation.rs#L109-L117](file://~/code/token-editor/src/csv/navigation.rs#L109-L117) - Triggers CSV viewport updates.
- [src/update/workspace.rs#L227-L270](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/workspace.rs#L227-L270) - `ensure_selection_visible` for the file tree/sidebar viewport.

The `Minimal` mode when cursor is `off_below` uses:
```rust
line + padding + 1 - self.viewport.visible_lines
```

This places the cursor at `visible_lines - padding - 1` from the top, which is the bottom of the safe zone. With padding=1 and visible_lines=35:
- Cursor would be at visual position 35 - 1 - 1 = 33 from top
- That's 33/35 = 94% down the viewport, definitely not centered!

So either:
1. The `visible_lines` value is wrong (much smaller than actual)
2. Or there's some other code path being hit

## Assistant

Current `scroll_padding = 1`, which should mean only 1 line of buffer. You want ~2 lines. But the issue is it scrolls **too much**, not too little.

Let me look at the `ensure_cursor_visible_with_mode` logic more carefully. With `padding = 1`:
- `safe_bottom = top_line + visible_lines - 1 - 1 = top_line + visible_lines - 2`

So the last 2 lines trigger scrolling. But when scrolling, where does the cursor end up?

```rust
// Cursor below safe zone: scroll down to put cursor at bottom margin
line + padding + 1 - self.viewport.visible_lines
```

With `padding = 1`, `line = 50`, `visible_lines = 35`:
- `new_top = 50 + 1 + 1 - 35 = 17`
- Cursor visual position = 50 - 17 = 33 (from top)
- That's `visible_lines - padding - 1 = 33`, i.e. cursor is at the bottom padding boundary

That's correct behavior for `padding = 1`. If it's scrolling too much, maybe `visible_lines` is smaller than expected?

## Assistant

`HoverRegion` is used for cursor tracking and scroll routing - it's different from `HitTarget` which is for click dispatch. They serve complementary purposes:
- `HoverRegion` - tracks where the mouse is currently hovering (for cursor icon and scroll routing)
- `HitTarget` - determines what was clicked (for click event dispatch)

These could potentially be unified, but they're both actively used and serve distinct purposes.

## Assistant

Fixed. The bug was that `EventResult` only carried redraw/focus information but not the actual `Cmd` returned by `update()`. When double-clicking a file in the sidebar, the `Cmd::Batch([Cmd::Redraw, Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse])` was being discarded.

**Summary of fix:**
- Added `cmd: Option<Cmd>` field to `EventResult::Consumed`
- Added `consumed_with_cmd()` helper to create results with commands
- Updated sidebar item double-click handler to capture and return the command
- Updated `handle_mouse_press` to use the command from `EventResult` if present
